{"parentUuid":"23d779cb-6460-4f59-aec7-be86f4ea0515","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"a11cbfc5-5b1b-4a8a-90ff-e4cf512e7689","agentId":"af0fe4f4b572e87a8","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KdXkngSbvytHnDvwzyKKBy","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Kimi's take: optimise on chains, not slots (2026-08-04)\n2\t\n3\tVerbatim response from Kimi (K2.6 Instant) to `docs/kimi_prompt_trajectory_optimisation.md`.\n4\tSaved before assessment so the source is on disk and not paraphrased away.\n5\tOur assessment follows at the bottom, clearly separated.\n6\t\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t## 1. The Core Shift: Mutate Templates, Not Actions\n10\t\n11\tA chain is a parameterized subroutine that expands into a coherent block of raw actions.\n12\tYour mutation operator should be:\n13\t\n14\t    mutate(template_type, start_day, parameters)\n15\t\n16\tNot:\n17\t\n18\t    mutate(slot_idx, new_action)\n19\t\n20\t### Example Chain Templates\n21\t\n22\t| Template | Parameters | Expands To |\n23\t|---|---|---|\n24\t| `LivestockChain` | `(start_day, animal, count, pasture_loc)` | Buy animal → build pasture → walk unit → pick up → place → count × feed loop → sell at maturity |\n25\t| `CropIntensive` | `(start_day, crop, plots, unit_ids)` | Buy seeds → plow → plant → water schedule → harvest schedule |\n26\t| `MarketArbitrage` | `(day, product, qty, expected_price)` | Sell/buy order sequence |\n27\t| `Expansion` | `(day, land_count)` | Save money → buy land → reallocate units |\n28\t\n29\tA single mutation now touches 30–200 raw slots coherently.\n30\t\n31\t## 2. How to Build the Chain Library\n32\t\n33\tYou do not need to hand-write every chain from scratch. Extract them from your data:\n34\t\n35\t**A. Replay Differencing.**\n36\tTake your seed trajectory ($23.8k) and a top-player trajectory ($145k).\n37\tAlign them day-by-day.\n38\tFind contiguous blocks where the top player is doing something your seed is not.\n39\tExtract those blocks as candidate chains.\n40\t\n41\t**B. Prefix Profit Analysis.**\n42\tReplay your seed and compute the exact marginal bank balance per turn.\n43\tLook for long sequences where money is locked up (negative cash flow) followed by a large payoff.\n44\tThose are chains.\n45\tThe `BUILD_PASTURE → BUY_COW → ... → SELL_BEEF` sequence will show up as a deep V-shaped cash-flow pattern.\n46\t\n47\t**C. Hand-Write the Obvious Ones.**\n48\tYou know livestock is 35% of leader revenue and your seed has zero.\n49\tHand-write a `LivestockChain` template with parameters `(start_day, cow_count)`.\n50\tThat alone is probably worth +$20k if placed correctly.\n51\t\n52\t## 3. The Search Algorithm: Forward Locking with Chain Insertion\n53\t\n54\tSince your simulator is deterministic and prefix-replayable, the most powerful technique is forward\n55\tlocking.\n56\tDo not optimize the full 720-turn script at once.\n57\tBuild it prefix-by-prefix.\n58\t\n59\t```python\n60\tbest_script = seed_script  # $23.8k baseline\n61\t\n62\tfor phase_start in [0, 30, 60, 90, ..., 690]:\n63\t    phase_end = phase_start + 30\n64\t\n65\t    # 1. Cache the exact state at phase_start from best_script\n66\t    cached_state = simulator.run(best_script[:phase_start])\n67\t\n68\t    # 2. Generate candidates: best_script with ONE chain inserted in [phase_start:phase_end]\n69\t    candidates = []\n70\t    for chain in chain_library:\n71\t        for start_day in range(phase_start, phase_end - chain.min_duration):\n72\t            mutated = best_script.copy()\n73\t            mutated[start_day : start_day+len(chain)] = chain.expand()\n74\t            candidates.append(mutated)\n75\t\n76\t    # 3. Evaluate from cached_state (not from turn 0)\n77\t    scores = jax.vmap(lambda s: simulator.run_from_state(cached_state, s[phase_start:]))(candidates)\n78\t\n79\t    # 4. Keep the best full script (may be best_script itself if no improvement)\n80\t    best_idx = jnp.argmax(scores)\n81\t    if scores[best_idx] > current_best_score:\n82\t        best_script = candidates[best_idx]\n83\t        current_best_score = scores[best_idx]\n84\t```\n85\t\n86\tWhy this works:\n87\t\n88\t- You evaluate a chain in the exact context it will run in.\n89\t- Prefix caching makes each eval ~30x cheaper than a full rollout.\n90\t- You are never trying to optimize day 500 while day 5 is still garbage.\n91\t- If a chain requires 25 days to pay off, you see the payoff within the phase (or the next phase).\n92\t\n93\t**Neutral Drift Variant.**\n94\tIf all candidates score worse than the baseline (common when a chain requires a setup turn that\n95\tlooks like a pure cost), allow temporary regression:\n96\t\n97\t- Keep a Pareto frontier of `(early_money, late_money)` or `(money_at_day_30, money_at_day_720)`.\n98\t- A chain that drops day-30 money but raises day-720 money is accepted.\n99\t\n100\t## 4. Chain Evaluation: Exact Finite Differences\n101\t\n102\tYour superpower is exact replay.\n103\tUse it to compute the exact value of a chain rather than noisy sampling.\n104\t\n105\t```python\n106\tdef chain_marginal_value(chain, base_script, insertion_day):\n107\t    state_t = simulator.run_prefix(base_script, insertion_day)\n108\t    baseline_final = simulator.run_from_state(state_t, base_script[insertion_day:])\n109\t\n110\t    variant_script = base_script.copy()\n111\t    variant_script[insertion_day : insertion_day+len(chain)] = chain.expand()\n112\t    variant_final = simulator.run_from_state(state_t, variant_script[insertion_day:])\n113\t\n114\t    return variant_final - baseline_final  # Exact, noiseless\n115\t```\n116\t\n117\tSince this is exact, you can rank every chain in your library at every possible insertion point and\n118\tpick the top-K to actually test.\n119\tThis is a pre-filter that costs almost nothing.\n120\t\n121\t## 5. Handling Chain Interactions (The Hard Part)\n122\t\n123\tChains compete.\n124\t`LivestockChain(day=5, cows=3)` and `CropIntensive(day=5, plots=10)` both need money and units.\n125\t\n126\t**A. Resource Budgeting.**\n127\tAdd a virtual resource tracker to your chain templates.\n128\tEach chain declares its peak cash need and unit usage.\n129\tBefore evaluating a candidate, check if the budget is feasible given the prefix state.\n130\tIf not, skip the eval.\n131\t\n132\t**B. Co-Evolution of Chain Sets.**\n133\tInstead of inserting one chain at a time, maintain a genome of chain slots:\n134\t\n135\t    Genome = [(chain_id, start_day, params), (chain_id, start_day, params), ...]\n136\t\n137\tThe decoder expands all chains into the script, resolving conflicts (e.g. two chains asking for the\n138\tsame unit on the same day) with a priority rule.\n139\tNow your search is over which chains to include and when, not over raw actions.\n140\t\n141\tUse a GA with crossover that swaps entire chain tuples between parents.\n142\tA child inherits Parent A's livestock schedule and Parent B's crop schedule.\n143\tThis respects the chain structure.\n144\t\n145\t## 6. The Closed-Loop Test (Do This Now)\n146\t\n147\tBefore you sink compute into chain optimization, run the falsification experiment:\n148\t\n149\t1. Take your current best open-loop script.\n150\t2. Train a tiny closed-loop policy (even a decision tree or 32-unit MLP) by behavioral cloning on it.\n151\t3. Fine-tune with exact rollouts against your 4 opponents.\n152\t4. Does it beat the open-loop script?\n153\t\n154\tIf yes, your chain optimization should target closed-loop chain templates (e.g. a\n155\t`ReactiveMarketChain` that reads current prices).\n156\tIf no, proceed with open-loop chains.\n157\t\n158\t## 7. Concrete Next Steps (Priority Order)\n159\t\n160\t| Step | Action | Expected Outcome |\n161\t|---|---|---|\n162\t| 1 | Hand-write `LivestockChain(start_day, count)` and `CropIntensive(start_day, crop, plots)` | Immediate +$10–30k if placed well |\n163\t| 2 | Implement prefix-cached forward locking (30-day phases) | Escape local optima by building coherent prefixes |\n164\t| 3 | Extract 5–10 candidate chains from top-player replay diffs | Data-driven chain discovery |\n165\t| 4 | Run exact marginal ranking of all chain x day combinations against your seed | Know exactly where to place chains |\n166\t| 5 | Switch to GA with chain-tuple crossover on a population of 500 | Combine livestock + crop + market chains |\n167\t| 6 | Run the closed-loop falsification test | Validate or invalidate the open-loop premise |\n168\t\n169\t## Bottom Line\n170\t\n171\tOptimizing on chains means your genome is a build order, not an action list.\n172\tYou are playing StarCraft, not tic-tac-toe.\n173\tThe right frame is real-time strategy search: find the best composition and timing of economic build\n174\torders, then let a compiler resolve them into unit-level actions.\n175\t\n176\tYour seed has zero livestock.\n177\tOne well-placed `LivestockChain` is probably worth more than 1,000 iterations of single-slot\n178\thill-climbing.\n179\tStart there.\n180\t\n181\t---\n182\t\n183\t# Our assessment (2026-08-04, after implementing `kagg/search/routines.py`)\n184\t\n185\t## Confirmed by measurement\n186\t\n187\tThe core shift is right and is now built and green (`tests/test_routines.py`, 8 passed).\n188\tOne COW in the `(4,4)` shed cell, measured in our own simulator:\n189\t\n190\t    do-nothing baseline      $ 3,000\n191\t    1x GOOSE on (4,4)        $ 4,604   net  +$1,604\n192\t    1x COW   on (4,4)        $11,119   net  +$8,119\n193\t    1x SHEEP on (4,4)        $ 9,510   net  +$6,510\n194\t\n195\t**Section 4 (exact finite differences via prefix caching) is the strongest idea in the take** and is\n196\tindependently correct for a reason Kimi did not know -- see the daily reset below, which makes day\n197\tboundaries exact resynchronisation points.\n198\t\n199\t## Where the take is wrong on the game's actual rules\n200\t\n201\t1. **\"count x feed loop\" and \"sell at maturity\" understate the obligation.**\n202\t   `consecutive_unfed >= 2` escapes the animal (`kaggriculture.py:786`).\n203\t   Feeding is a *daily* commitment for the rest of the season, not a bounded loop.\n204\t   And `pending_care_bonus` accumulates +1 per fed+cared day and dumps entirely on the next\n205\t   production day capped at `max_held`, so daily harvest is near-optimal and a single sale at\n206\t   maturity throws most of the yield away.\n207\t\n208\t2. **\"+$20k from livestock\" is not supported.**\n209\t   There are only four shed-access tiles `(4,4) (5,4) (4,5) (5,5)`, and they are the only tiles where\n210\t   PICKUP/DROP work.\n211\t   The zero-movement ranch therefore caps at four animals.\n212\t   Our measured +$8,119 is also an *upper bound*: it was scored against a do-nothing opponent, and\n213\t   the reason it is that large is that `_town_consume` drains market inventory while nothing refills\n214\t   it, so MILK climbed $212 -> $333 across the season.\n215\t   A real opponent selling milk removes most of that.\n216\t\n217\t3. **Turn/day units are conflated.** `phase_start in [0, 30, ..., 690]` over a 720-*turn* script is\n218\t   30-turn phases, i.e. 1.25 days at `turnsPerDay=24`. The natural phase is the day.\n219\t\n220\t4. **Forward locking has a specific failure here: land.**\n221\t   `LAND_ORDER` is `['NE','SW','SE']` at `[1000, 2000, 4000]`, bought strictly in sequence, and\n222\t   `_do_buy_land` silently no-ops when money is short.\n223\t   Saving for a $4,000 purchase looks exactly like doing nothing to a prefix-greedy optimiser, so\n224\t   forward locking will never fund it.\n225\t   Land timing has to be a global plan parameter, not a locally discovered one.\n226\t   Kimi's Pareto variant helps but does not fix this.\n227\t\n228\t5. **The closed-loop falsification test is partly already answered.**\n229\t   Across leader replays, market actions are **720/720 identical** between episodes against\n230\t   different opponents while unit actions differ on 6 of 30 days\n231\t   (`data/logs/open_loop_subin.log`).\n232\t   The market half of the artifact is demonstrably open-loop already.\n233\t"}]},"uuid":"045c365b-f943-4422-aeb6-1e3cc0af7207","timestamp":"2026-08-04T09:25:55.320Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/docs/kimi_take_chain_optimisation_2026-08-04.md","content":"# Kimi's take: optimise on chains, not slots (2026-08-04)\n\nVerbatim response from Kimi (K2.6 Instant) to `docs/kimi_prompt_trajectory_optimisation.md`.\nSaved before assessment so the source is on disk and not paraphrased away.\nOur assessment follows at the bottom, clearly separated.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The Core Shift: Mutate Templates, Not Actions\n\nA chain is a parameterized subroutine that expands into a coherent block of raw actions.\nYour mutation operator should be:\n\n    mutate(template_type, start_day, parameters)\n\nNot:\n\n    mutate(slot_idx, new_action)\n\n### Example Chain Templates\n\n| Template | Parameters | Expands To |\n|---|---|---|\n| `LivestockChain` | `(start_day, animal, count, pasture_loc)` | Buy animal → build pasture → walk unit → pick up → place → count × feed loop → sell at maturity |\n| `CropIntensive` | `(start_day, crop, plots, unit_ids)` | Buy seeds → plow → plant → water schedule → harvest schedule |\n| `MarketArbitrage` | `(day, product, qty, expected_price)` | Sell/buy order sequence |\n| `Expansion` | `(day, land_count)` | Save money → buy land → reallocate units |\n\nA single mutation now touches 30–200 raw slots coherently.\n\n## 2. How to Build the Chain Library\n\nYou do not need to hand-write every chain from scratch. Extract them from your data:\n\n**A. Replay Differencing.**\nTake your seed trajectory ($23.8k) and a top-player trajectory ($145k).\nAlign them day-by-day.\nFind contiguous blocks where the top player is doing something your seed is not.\nExtract those blocks as candidate chains.\n\n**B. Prefix Profit Analysis.**\nReplay your seed and compute the exact marginal bank balance per turn.\nLook for long sequences where money is locked up (negative cash flow) followed by a large payoff.\nThose are chains.\nThe `BUILD_PASTURE → BUY_COW → ... → SELL_BEEF` sequence will show up as a deep V-shaped cash-flow pattern.\n\n**C. Hand-Write the Obvious Ones.**\nYou know livestock is 35% of leader revenue and your seed has zero.\nHand-write a `LivestockChain` template with parameters `(start_day, cow_count)`.\nThat alone is probably worth +$20k if placed correctly.\n\n## 3. The Search Algorithm: Forward Locking with Chain Insertion\n\nSince your simulator is deterministic and prefix-replayable, the most powerful technique is forward\nlocking.\nDo not optimize the full 720-turn script at once.\nBuild it prefix-by-prefix.\n\n```python\nbest_script = seed_script  # $23.8k baseline\n\nfor phase_start in [0, 30, 60, 90, ..., 690]:\n    phase_end = phase_start + 30\n\n    # 1. Cache the exact state at phase_start from best_script\n    cached_state = simulator.run(best_script[:phase_start])\n\n    # 2. Generate candidates: best_script with ONE chain inserted in [phase_start:phase_end]\n    candidates = []\n    for chain in chain_library:\n        for start_day in range(phase_start, phase_end - chain.min_duration):\n            mutated = best_script.copy()\n            mutated[start_day : start_day+len(chain)] = chain.expand()\n            candidates.append(mutated)\n\n    # 3. Evaluate from cached_state (not from turn 0)\n    scores = jax.vmap(lambda s: simulator.run_from_state(cached_state, s[phase_start:]))(candidates)\n\n    # 4. Keep the best full script (may be best_script itself if no improvement)\n    best_idx = jnp.argmax(scores)\n    if scores[best_idx] > current_best_score:\n        best_script = candidates[best_idx]\n        current_best_score = scores[best_idx]\n```\n\nWhy this works:\n\n- You evaluate a chain in the exact context it will run in.\n- Prefix caching makes each eval ~30x cheaper than a full rollout.\n- You are never trying to optimize day 500 while day 5 is still garbage.\n- If a chain requires 25 days to pay off, you see the payoff within the phase (or the next phase).\n\n**Neutral Drift Variant.**\nIf all candidates score worse than the baseline (common when a chain requires a setup turn that\nlooks like a pure cost), allow temporary regression:\n\n- Keep a Pareto frontier of `(early_money, late_money)` or `(money_at_day_30, money_at_day_720)`.\n- A chain that drops day-30 money but raises day-720 money is accepted.\n\n## 4. Chain Evaluation: Exact Finite Differences\n\nYour superpower is exact replay.\nUse it to compute the exact value of a chain rather than noisy sampling.\n\n```python\ndef chain_marginal_value(chain, base_script, insertion_day):\n    state_t = simulator.run_prefix(base_script, insertion_day)\n    baseline_final = simulator.run_from_state(state_t, base_script[insertion_day:])\n\n    variant_script = base_script.copy()\n    variant_script[insertion_day : insertion_day+len(chain)] = chain.expand()\n    variant_final = simulator.run_from_state(state_t, variant_script[insertion_day:])\n\n    return variant_final - baseline_final  # Exact, noiseless\n```\n\nSince this is exact, you can rank every chain in your library at every possible insertion point and\npick the top-K to actually test.\nThis is a pre-filter that costs almost nothing.\n\n## 5. Handling Chain Interactions (The Hard Part)\n\nChains compete.\n`LivestockChain(day=5, cows=3)` and `CropIntensive(day=5, plots=10)` both need money and units.\n\n**A. Resource Budgeting.**\nAdd a virtual resource tracker to your chain templates.\nEach chain declares its peak cash need and unit usage.\nBefore evaluating a candidate, check if the budget is feasible given the prefix state.\nIf not, skip the eval.\n\n**B. Co-Evolution of Chain Sets.**\nInstead of inserting one chain at a time, maintain a genome of chain slots:\n\n    Genome = [(chain_id, start_day, params), (chain_id, start_day, params), ...]\n\nThe decoder expands all chains into the script, resolving conflicts (e.g. two chains asking for the\nsame unit on the same day) with a priority rule.\nNow your search is over which chains to include and when, not over raw actions.\n\nUse a GA with crossover that swaps entire chain tuples between parents.\nA child inherits Parent A's livestock schedule and Parent B's crop schedule.\nThis respects the chain structure.\n\n## 6. The Closed-Loop Test (Do This Now)\n\nBefore you sink compute into chain optimization, run the falsification experiment:\n\n1. Take your current best open-loop script.\n2. Train a tiny closed-loop policy (even a decision tree or 32-unit MLP) by behavioral cloning on it.\n3. Fine-tune with exact rollouts against your 4 opponents.\n4. Does it beat the open-loop script?\n\nIf yes, your chain optimization should target closed-loop chain templates (e.g. a\n`ReactiveMarketChain` that reads current prices).\nIf no, proceed with open-loop chains.\n\n## 7. Concrete Next Steps (Priority Order)\n\n| Step | Action | Expected Outcome |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Hand-write `LivestockChain(start_day, count)` and `CropIntensive(start_day, crop, plots)` | Immediate +$10–30k if placed well |\n| 2 | Implement prefix-cached forward locking (30-day phases) | Escape local optima by building coherent prefixes |\n| 3 | Extract 5–10 candidate chains from top-player replay diffs | Data-driven chain discovery |\n| 4 | Run exact marginal ranking of all chain x day combinations against your seed | Know exactly where to place chains |\n| 5 | Switch to GA with chain-tuple crossover on a population of 500 | Combine livestock + crop + market chains |\n| 6 | Run the closed-loop falsification test | Validate or invalidate the open-loop premise |\n\n## Bottom Line\n\nOptimizing on chains means your genome is a build order, not an action list.\nYou are playing StarCraft, not tic-tac-toe.\nThe right frame is real-time strategy search: find the best composition and timing of economic build\norders, then let a compiler resolve them into unit-level actions.\n\nYour seed has zero livestock.\nOne well-placed `LivestockChain` is probably worth more than 1,000 iterations of single-slot\nhill-climbing.\nStart there.\n\n---\n\n# Our assessment (2026-08-04, after implementing `kagg/search/routines.py`)\n\n## Confirmed by measurement\n\nThe core shift is right and is now built and green (`tests/test_routines.py`, 8 passed).\nOne COW in the `(4,4)` shed cell, measured in our own simulator:\n\n    do-nothing baseline      $ 3,000\n    1x GOOSE on (4,4)        $ 4,604   net  +$1,604\n    1x COW   on (4,4)        $11,119   net  +$8,119\n    1x SHEEP on (4,4)        $ 9,510   net  +$6,510\n\n**Section 4 (exact finite differences via prefix caching) is the strongest idea in the take** and is\nindependently correct for a reason Kimi did not know -- see the daily reset below, which makes day\nboundaries exact resynchronisation points.\n\n## Where the take is wrong on the game's actual rules\n\n1. **\"count x feed loop\" and \"sell at maturity\" understate the obligation.**\n   `consecutive_unfed >= 2` escapes the animal (`kaggriculture.py:786`).\n   Feeding is a *daily* commitment for the rest of the season, not a bounded loop.\n   And `pending_care_bonus` accumulates +1 per fed+cared day and dumps entirely on the next\n   production day capped at `max_held`, so daily harvest is near-optimal and a single sale at\n   maturity throws most of the yield away.\n\n2. **\"+$20k from livestock\" is not supported.**\n   There are only four shed-access tiles `(4,4) (5,4) (4,5) (5,5)`, and they are the only tiles where\n   PICKUP/DROP work.\n   The zero-movement ranch therefore caps at four animals.\n   Our measured +$8,119 is also an *upper bound*: it was scored against a do-nothing opponent, and\n   the reason it is that large is that `_town_consume` drains market inventory while nothing refills\n   it, so MILK climbed $212 -> $333 across the season.\n   A real opponent selling milk removes most of that.\n\n3. **Turn/day units are conflated.** `phase_start in [0, 30, ..., 690]` over a 720-*turn* script is\n   30-turn phases, i.e. 1.25 days at `turnsPerDay=24`. The natural phase is the day.\n\n4. **Forward locking has a specific failure here: land.**\n   `LAND_ORDER` is `['NE','SW','SE']` at `[1000, 2000, 4000]`, bought strictly in sequence, and\n   `_do_buy_land` silently no-ops when money is short.\n   Saving for a $4,000 purchase looks exactly like doing nothing to a prefix-greedy optimiser, so\n   forward locking will never fund it.\n   Land timing has to be a global plan parameter, not a locally discovered one.\n   Kimi's Pareto variant helps but does not fix this.\n\n5. **The closed-loop falsification test is partly already answered.**\n   Across leader replays, market actions are **720/720 identical** between episodes against\n   different opponents while unit actions differ on 6 of 30 days\n   (`data/logs/open_loop_subin.log`).\n   The market half of the artifact is demonstrably open-loop already.\n","numLines":233,"startLine":1,"totalLines":233}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"23d779cb-6460-4f59-aec7-be86f4ea0515","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"6e91a562-2c58-4e1d-8c6f-dd43634ac84a","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"humming-hopping-marshmallow"}

{"parentUuid":"6b6dd0fe-658e-44eb-a648-9a2c11e4eee5","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"af0fe4f4b572e87a8","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5","id":"msg_011CdhU83u3tnkWdyURtqoXr","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read `reference/kaggriculture.py` in full (all 1054 lines), `kagg/search/routines.py`, `tests/test_routines.py`, and the Kimi doc. Answers below, in order, with the reference lines that ground each claim.\n\n## 1. Genome design: chain-tuple, not day-slots — but the *search* is day-indexed\n\nKimi's `[(chain_id, start_day, params), ...]` decodes more cleanly, for a reason neither of you stated: a routine like `ranch()` already writes ops for every day from `start_day` to day 29 (`routines.py:195-209`). A 30-slot-per-day genome would need to re-express \"still feeding the cow\" as content in 29 separate slots, which is redundant and creates exactly the fragility `routines.py`'s own docstring warns about for raw op tapes (`routines.py:25-27`): touching slot 15 could look independent of slot 3 while actually describing the same running chain, so mutation/crossover would need to know slots belong together anyway. A chain-tuple is the natural indivisible gene; don't fight that.\n\nBut don't conflate genome *shape* with the *search algorithm*. The thing that actually has the Markov structure you want is the day boundary (`_end_of_day`, ref line 829), not the tuple list. So: genome = list of chain instances, search = a day-indexed beam/DP that, at each day, only asks \"which new chains can legally start today, given the real simulator state\" — never a virtual resource-budget estimate (Kimi §5A). You have the real simulator; use it. Concretely:\n\n```\nChainInstance = {chain_type, start_day, unit_id, tile, params}\ngenome = [ChainInstance, ...]          # unordered set, sorted by start_day at decode time\n\ndecode(genome) -> Script:\n    for instance in sorted(genome, key=start_day):\n        routine_builder[instance.chain_type](script, **instance)   # ranch(), crop_block(), ...\n    bake in the evergreen BUY_LAND standing order unconditionally (see Q3)\n```\n\nSearch loop, one iteration per day, operating on live simulator state not the tape:\n\n```\nbeam = [initial_state]\nfor day in range(30):\n    candidates = []\n    for node in beam:\n        for choice in legal_new_chains(node.sim_state, day, chain_library):  # incl. \"nothing new\"\n            new_state = simulate_one_day(node.sim_state, decode_day(node.genome + [choice]), opponent)\n            candidates.append(Node(new_state, node.genome + [choice]))\n    beam = diversity_top_k(candidates, value_fn, K)\n```\n\n## 2. Yes — it's DP, and I checked the Markov claim by reading `_end_of_day` rather than trusting it\n\nI verified line-by-line what survives the daily reset (ref 829-851): `money`, `tiles` (crops+animals+weeds+structures with all counters), `unlocked_quadrants`, `private[\"shed\"]`, `private[\"seeds\"]` (not reset — you missed this in your list, it's a small state-vector gap, not a Markov violation), the shared `market`, and `town[\"unlocked_shops\"]`. Farmer/hand position, hands list, and `hires_today` are wiped. Town-shop unlock and weed spawn are RNG-driven by `(seed, day)` only (ref 838-840, 855-859) — exogenous, not action-dependent, so they don't hide state either. **Your Markov claim holds.** This is legitimately DP, not stochastic search, provided the opponent is a fixed function of state (heuristic or scripted) so `simulate_one_day` is deterministic given the genome choice.\n\nWhere I disagree with your framing: don't look for an admissible heuristic — there isn't one, and chasing one is the wrong tool here. Do what MCTS/AlphaZero do when no admissible heuristic exists: **rank beam nodes by a rollout, not by current money.** From each candidate state, roll a fixed, decent auto-pilot forward (say H=7-10 days, not all 30 — full-horizon rollout for every beam node every day is O(days²) and unnecessary) in the same vmapped JAX sim, and score by the resulting money/win-margin. That directly prices in the option value of \"$500 cash + 1 live COW ranch\" vs \"$4000 cash + nothing,\" because the rollout actually cashes the COW in. It's not admissible in the A* sense, but it's cheap, exact-per-draw, and it is precisely the thing your CLAUDE.md already does for RL (dense proxy signal during search, but **select final winners on win-rate against the opponent pool, not money** — that rule doesn't stop applying just because the search changed).\n\nTwo more concrete failure modes to guard against:\n- **Beam collapse on land timing.** Pure top-K by rollout value will converge all K nodes onto the same land-purchase-day hypothesis within a few iterations, because short-horizon rollouts undervalue \"save now\" (see Q3). Cluster/bucket the beam by land-purchase-day vector and animal-portfolio signature before pruning, or you'll never get to compare timing alternatives.\n- **Two-stage filter for cost.** Use raw money (cheap) to cut candidates to ~3K, then spend the rollout budget only on those.\n\n## 3. Land funding: fix it in the decoder, don't search for it\n\nI checked `_do_buy_land` (ref 680-693) and the order-processing loop (ref 519-556, 605-623) directly. Two facts make this decisive, not just \"principled\":\n\n1. `_do_buy_land` silently no-ops on insufficient money and costs nothing to attempt — confirmed, no side effect at all on failure.\n2. Atomic orders (`HIRE`, `BUY_LAND`) at queue position *i* are resolved **before** the per-unit lockstep buy/sell at that same position *i* (ref 546-556 runs before 558-602). So an order list with `BUY_LAND` at slot 0 gets first claim on your own cash that turn, ahead of anything else you queue that day.\n\nThat means land timing is not a search problem at all — it's a decoder bug waiting to be fixed. **Emit `[\"BUY_LAND\"]` at order-slot 0 on every single turn, from turn 0, unconditionally, forever** (or until all three are bought). It fires the instant you cross the threshold, it never costs anything to attempt, and by construction the search can never \"forget\" to try. This is stronger than Kimi's Pareto-frontier fix, which tolerates the failure mode (accepts temporary regression); the decoder change removes the failure mode. It's the same move `routines.py` already made for the animal chain — \"a partial chain cannot be expressed\" (`routines.py:16-19`) — applied to land: a plan that could have bought land but didn't queue it shouldn't be expressible either.\n\nWhat's left for the *search* to actually decide, once this is baked in, is much smaller: whether to throttle other spending so land isn't starved. Given slot-0 priority already guarantees land wins any same-turn cash contention, I don't think you need to expose anything further here — measure first before adding a knob.\n\n## 4. Prior risk: the library is narrower than it is strategic — that's the real cap\n\nChecked `ranch()` against your own test (routines.py:137-211, tests/test_routines.py). It parameterizes animal, tile, unit, start_day and leaves those to the search — that's the \"shape\" side, and it's fine. Two places it does encode a specific policy, both flagged in its own docstring as swappable, neither yet swappable:\n- fixed daily sell cadence (`sell[d+6] += max_held` every day, routines.py:206-209) — the docstring admits this (\"timing is a plan parameter the search should own\") but nothing exposes it yet.\n- animal chains only. No crop-intensive routine, no hire/crew-scaling routine, no market-arbitrage routine exist. Given the project's own measured finding that leaders drew 83% of revenue from converting bought wheat into animal products (CLAUDE.md kill-criteria section), a library that is 100% \"ranch\" and 0% everything else caps the ceiling by omission, not by being too prescriptive.\n\n**Concrete test for whether the library has capped the ceiling** (better than a vague \"is it a policy\" gut check): mine 5-10 day-level blocks from a real top-player replay (Kimi's method A, which you already have the data for per `data/logs/open_loop_subin.log`) and check whether each block is expressible as *some* combination of your existing chains at *some* parameters. Any block that isn't — e.g., simultaneous multi-tile crop rotation, wheat arbitrage via `BUY_PRODUCT`/`SELL` round-trips, hire-wave-then-scatter-to-crops — is a direct, measured coverage gap, not a matter of opinion. Do this before spending compute on the beam search; it tells you which chains to add first.\n\n## 5. What I think you mis-read or haven't noticed (adversarial, as asked)\n\n**(a) The first hand you hire every day spawns on a LOCKED tile if you haven't bought NE yet — and this silently paralyzes it.** `_spawn_hand` (ref 508-516) picks the least-occupied shed-access tile, ties broken by NWSE index, with **no check of that tile's lock status**. With the farmer defaulting to `(4,4)` (occupancy 1) and the other three cells at 0, the tie-break picks `(5,4)` — the **NE** cell — for the very first hand, every single day. `_apply_unit_action` checks `tile == \"LOCKED\"` and returns before reaching DROP/PICKUP/PLACE/FEED/CARE (ref 320-324, guards everything below it). So if NE isn't bought yet, that hand's entire day is void except moving off the tile. This directly contradicts the implicit assumption a caller might make when placing a hand-run ranch at a specific cell — `routines.py`'s docstring says \"a caller placing hands must supply the truth\" (line 146) but doesn't give the actual rule. The rule is: **hand #k (in fill order, one hire call at a time) spawns at `shed_access_tiles()[k]`** — which happens to be exactly `(4,4)→NE→SW→SE`, i.e. hand-hire order and `LAND_ORDER` are pre-aligned by the game's own design. Net effect: to run a hand-staffed ranch pen at NE/SW/SE, that parcel must already be bought before that hand's first working day, or the day is wasted. This reinforces Q3's land-timing finding with a second, independent mechanism (not just \"land unlocks tiles\" — \"land unlocks the ability of a hired unit to act at all on that tile\").\n\n**(b) The current `ranch()` routine leaves free money on the table: it never collects fertilizer.** Every surviving animal tile gets `fertilizer_available = True` at every day-end, unconditionally (ref `_daily_refresh_animals`, line 800), collectible via `COLLECT_FERTILIZER` (ref 490-497) from the same tile, same turn budget as FEED/CARE. `routines.py`'s `ranch()` never emits this op. So the measured +$8,119 (COW) / +$6,510 (SHEEP) / +$1,604 (GOOSE) figures are a lower bound on the chain's own value, not its ceiling — before any strategy search runs at all, the mechanics themselves aren't complete. Cheap fix: add `COLLECT_FERTILIZER` + include FERTILIZER in the DROP/sell path (or route it to a crop tile via `FERTILIZE` for the 2x yield window, ref 373-386).\n\n**(c) Shed capacity is global, not per-item — 100 units total across every product, animal-in-shed, and byproduct combined** (`_new_private`, ref 154-160: one `shed` dict summed across all of `PRODUCTS + list(ANIMALS)`; `_drop_inventories_to_shed`, ref 812-826, explicitly discards overflow with no error). This caps any \"hoard inventory, sell late for a better price\" strategy, which is otherwise incentivized by a real, measurable structural fact you haven't flagged: `_town_consume` (ref 696-718) drains market inventory on an accelerating schedule — more town shops unlock over the season (up to all 8, every `townShopUnlockInterval` days) and the town-center multiplier itself steps up at day 10 and day 20 (`TOWN_CENTER_DEMAND_SCHEDULE`, line 103) — so prices for nearly every product structurally drift upward late-season, independent of the opponent. That's a real, unexploited market-timing lever, but it's coupled to the shed cap: you can't just defer selling, you'll silently lose the overflow. Any \"sell late\" chain the library eventually grows needs to reason about this cap explicitly.\n\n**(d) Hiring is nearly free and scales crop coverage, not just ranches — and that's probably a bigger lever than more ranching.** Ranch pens are hard-capped at 4 (shed-tile-limited, as you noted), but crop tiles are not — once land is bought you have up to 100 tiles, and the Fibonacci hire ladder resets daily (ref 658-677), so an 8-hand crew costs ≈$1,600 over the season. The biggest unexploited lever combined with land is probably \"many cheap hands routed to crop tiles across all four quadrants,\" not a fifth animal — and your current library has no crop routine at all to test that with (see Q4).\n\nNone of this overturns your established facts — the daily reset, the animal-chain wall, and the 4-tile ranch cap are all confirmed by direct reading. But the ranch numbers you're using as a baseline are provably a lower bound (fertilizer), and two of the \"obviously true\" simplifications — \"land timing is discoverable locally\" and \"a hand will work wherever I put it\" — are false for specific, checkable mechanical reasons above."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":37870,"cache_read_input_tokens":39197,"output_tokens":31147,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":37870},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":31147,"cache_read_input_tokens":39197,"cache_creation_input_tokens":37870,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":37870,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdhU82cAGEqnkCgfL8eQ6","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"214d1a04-5ae8-423d-aa43-be9e4987e18c","timestamp":"2026-08-04T09:32:32.920Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"6e91a562-2c58-4e1d-8c6f-dd43634ac84a","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"humming-hopping-marshmallow"}

{"parentUuid":"2bde52c7-3402-498e-a400-7c39f40e10a0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bffb09b-9cfa-457a-b68e-4ca7752df84e","agentId":"af0fe4f4b572e87a8","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FCXJ4mKTPxJ299D56n5okh","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The search space: a *farm plan*, and its encoding as a vector of categorical genes.\n2\t\n3\tWhy a plan and not raw actions\n4\t------------------------------\n5\t\n6\tThe obvious thing to search on a 750k-step/sec simulator is the raw action sequence: 719 turns of\n7\t`Actions`, one op per unit plus a market order list. That space is hopeless and, worse, it is\n8\t*untransferable*. Three reasons, all specific to this environment:\n9\t\n10\t1. **Size.** 719 turns x 15 units x 9 ops is ~10^10000 sequences, with a single scalar at the end.\n11\t2. **It is not the same problem in the real environment.** An open-loop script assumes it knows\n12\t   where every unit is standing. The simulator spawns every hired hand on the NW shed tile while\n13\t   the reference balances them across all four (`kagg/sim/parity.py` documents this), and the real\n14\t   environment spawns weeds the simulator only matches statistically. An open-loop script that is\n15\t   optimal in the simulator desynchronises in the reference on turn one and never recovers.\n16\t3. **The interesting decisions are not per-turn.** What actually determines a season is which tile\n17\t   grows what, when it is planted, when it is cut, how many hands are paid for that day, and how\n18\t   much of the shed goes to market at what price. Those are ~30 decisions a season, not 10,000.\n19\t\n20\tSo the search space here is a **closed-loop plan** executed by a deliberately mechanical\n21\tinterpreter (`kagg.search.executor`). The plan carries all of the strategy; the executor carries\n22\tnone of it -- it walks a unit to the nearest tile in its block that has an outstanding job and does\n23\tthat job. Because the executor is closed-loop on the *observed* state, it self-corrects the hand\n24\tspawn divergence, weed spawns, and anything else the two implementations disagree about, and the\n25\tidentical code runs against both the batched simulator and a real observation dict.\n26\t\n27\tThis is deliberately *not* the heuristic's parameter vector. `scripts/tune_heuristic.py` tunes 17\n28\tdials on top of hand-written routing, crop priorities and sell rules; the experiment log calls that\n29\tlayer exhausted (\"ES tunes numbers; it cannot invent structure\"). Here there is no crop priority\n30\torder, no filler crop, no sell cadence and no hire curve to inherit -- every tile chooses its own\n31\tcrop and its own planting phase independently, every five-day block chooses its own sell rate per\n32\tproduct, and every day chooses its own headcount. 277 genes, initialised uniform.\n33\t\n34\tEncoding\n35\t--------\n36\t\n37\tEvery gene is a **categorical** index, not a float. Crop choice, planting phase and land timing have\n38\tno meaningful metric, and the ones that do (headcount, sell quantity) are strongly non-linear in\n39\ttheir effect. A factored categorical distribution -- one probability table per gene -- is therefore\n40\tthe natural CEM/EDA model, and it makes the learned distribution directly readable: after the\n41\tsearch, `p(gene=WHEAT)` for each tile *is* the answer to \"what should this tile grow\".\n42\t\n43\tGenes, in order:\n44\t\n45\t===================  ====  ======  ====================================================\n46\tname                 dims  values  meaning\n47\t===================  ====  ======  ====================================================\n48\t``crop_of``          100   6       crop for tile i, or FALLOW (never farm this tile)\n49\t``phase``            100   5       plant tile i only on days where ``day % 5 == phase``\n50\t``stop_plant``       1     8       last day on which anything is planted\n51\t``harvest_par``      5     5       per crop: how ripe to let it get before cutting\n52\t``hires``            30    8       target headcount for each day of the season\n53\t``sell_qty``         30    6       units offered per sell tick, per product per 5-day block\n54\t``sell_every``       1     6       hours between sell ticks\n55\t``floor_frac``       5     6       per product: refuse to sell below this fraction of base\n56\t``seed_reserve``     1     5       cash kept back from seed buying\n57\t``land_day``         3     9       earliest day each extra quadrant may be bought\n58\t``dump_day``         1     6       from this day, sell everything regardless of price\n59\t===================  ====  ======  ====================================================\n60\t\"\"\"\n61\t\n62\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n63\t\n64\timport numpy as np\n65\t\n66\tBOARD = 10\n67\tTILES = BOARD * BOARD\n68\tSEASON_DAYS = 30\n69\tN_BLOCKS = 6           # 5-day blocks, for the sell schedule\n70\tBLOCK_DAYS = 5\n71\t\n72\t# Crop order is `kaggle_environments.envs.kaggriculture.CROPS`, which is also the first five\n73\t# entries of PRODUCTS, so a crop id doubles as a product id.\n74\tCROP_NAMES = [\"WHEAT\", \"CARROT\", \"TOMATO\", \"STRAWBERRY\", \"MELON\"]\n75\tN_CROPS = len(CROP_NAMES)\n76\tFALLOW = -1\n77\t\n78\tBASE_PRICE = np.array([25.0, 35.0, 60.0, 120.0, 250.0])\n79\tSEED_COST = np.array([10.0, 20.0, 50.0, 100.0, 80.0])\n80\tFIRST_YIELD_DAY = np.array([2, 2, 8, 10, 10], dtype=np.int16)\n81\tMAX_YIELD_DAY = np.array([4, 3, 8, 10, 12], dtype=np.int16)\n82\tONGOING = np.array([False, False, True, True, False])\n83\t\n84\t# Gene value tables. Index into these with the categorical gene.\n85\tSTOP_PLANT_DAYS = np.array([14, 16, 18, 20, 22, 24, 26, 30], dtype=np.int16)\n86\t# For a crop that clears the tile when cut, how many days past `max_yield_day` to wait; for an\n87\t# ongoing crop, how many units to let pile up (its cap is 4) before cutting.\n88\tHARVEST_DELTA = np.array([-2, -1, 0, 1, 2], dtype=np.int16)\n89\tONGOING_THRESHOLD = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 4], dtype=np.int16)\n90\tHIRE_LEVELS = np.array([0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14], dtype=np.int16)\n91\tSELL_QTYS = np.array([0, 2, 5, 10, 25, 100], dtype=np.int32)\n92\tSELL_EVERY = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 12], dtype=np.int16)\n93\tFLOOR_FRACS = np.array([0.0, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1.1])\n94\tSEED_RESERVES = np.array([0.0, 200.0, 500.0, 1000.0, 2000.0])\n95\tNEVER = np.int16(999)\n96\tLAND_DAYS = np.array([0, 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21, NEVER], dtype=np.int16)\n97\tDUMP_DAYS = np.array([20, 22, 24, 26, 28, NEVER], dtype=np.int16)\n98\t\n99\t# ---- livestock -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n100\t#\n101\t# **Added 2026-08-04. The search space could not express the strategy that is winning.**\n102\t# `docs/episode_teardown_2026-08-03.md`: the top three agents all submit the same plan -- COW 8,\n103\t# SHEEP 6, GOOSE 0, 14 pastures, 0 coops, 967 units of wheat bought as feed -- and draw 34.9% of\n104\t# revenue from milk and wool plus ~6% from reselling fertiliser collected free off the animals.\n105\t# `crop_of` had six categories, five crops and fallow, so a tile could not hold a structure and CEM\n106\t# was searching the crop-only subspace we have already measured losing.\n107\t\n108\t# A tile's *use* is one decision, so structures are extra `crop_of` categories rather than a second\n109\t# competing gene block. Gene values: 0 = fallow, 1..5 = crop, 6 = coop, 7 = pasture.\n110\tCROP_GENE_COOP = N_CROPS + 1\n111\tCROP_GENE_PASTURE = N_CROPS + 2\n112\tNO_STRUCTURE, COOP, PASTURE = 0, 1, 2\n113\t\n114\tANIMAL_NAMES = [\"GOOSE\", \"COW\", \"SHEEP\"]\n115\tN_ANIMALS = len(ANIMAL_NAMES)\n116\t\n117\t# Target headcount per species. The measured meta is COW 8 / SHEEP 6, so the ladder brackets both\n118\t# and reaches well past them -- a search that cannot exceed the known point cannot improve on it.\n119\tHERD_LEVELS = np.array([0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16], dtype=np.int16)\n120\t\n121\t# Day to start stocking. Animals pay from `ANIMAL_FIRST_YIELD_DAY` (goose 4, sheep 6, cow 8) and a\n122\t# structure is free to build (`jax_sim.py:613`), so the only cost of going early is the cash.\n123\tSTOCK_DAYS = np.array([0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, NEVER], dtype=np.int16)\n124\t\n125\t# Units of wheat to hold *per animal* for feeding. `jax_masks.py:96` gates FEED on `carrying_wheat`,\n126\t# and an animal unfed two nights escapes with the whole purchase price. The leaders bought 967 units\n127\t# for 14 animals over a season.\n128\tFEED_WHEAT = np.array([0, 1, 2, 4, 8], dtype=np.int16)\n129\t\n130\t# What to do with fertiliser. COLLECT_FERTILIZER is free -- no action cost, one per animal per day --\n131\t# and FERTILIZE doubles a watered plant's yield for three days (`jax_sim.py:1096`). The leaders do\n132\t# both: 318 collects, 107 fertilises, and 406 units sold for ~$21,900.\n133\tFERT_SELL, FERT_APPLY, FERT_APPLY_THEN_SELL = 0, 1, 2\n134\tN_FERT_POLICIES = 3\n135\t\n136\t# (name, number of genes, number of categories)\n137\t# **Append-only.** Inserting a block would repoint every gene after it and silently change what\n138\t# `best_plan.json` means -- `agent.py` loads that file as the default plan. `pad_genome` migrates a\n139\t# genome saved before the livestock blocks existed, and `tests/test_plan_animals.py` pins that it\n140\t# decodes to exactly the same plan it did before.\n141\tSPEC = (\n142\t    (\"crop_of\", TILES, N_CROPS + 3),          # + coop, + pasture\n143\t    (\"phase\", TILES, 5),\n144\t    (\"stop_plant\", 1, len(STOP_PLANT_DAYS)),\n145\t    (\"harvest_par\", N_CROPS, len(HARVEST_DELTA)),\n146\t    (\"hires\", SEASON_DAYS, len(HIRE_LEVELS)),\n147\t    (\"sell_qty\", N_BLOCKS * N_CROPS, len(SELL_QTYS)),\n148\t    (\"sell_every\", 1, len(SELL_EVERY)),\n149\t    (\"floor_frac\", N_CROPS, len(FLOOR_FRACS)),\n150\t    (\"seed_reserve\", 1, len(SEED_RESERVES)),\n151\t    (\"land_day\", 3, len(LAND_DAYS)),\n152\t    (\"dump_day\", 1, len(DUMP_DAYS)),\n153\t    # --- livestock, appended 2026-08-04 ---\n154\t    (\"herd\", N_ANIMALS, len(HERD_LEVELS)),\n155\t    (\"stock_day\", 1, len(STOCK_DAYS)),\n156\t    (\"feed_wheat\", 1, len(FEED_WHEAT)),\n157\t    (\"fert_policy\", 1, N_FERT_POLICIES),\n158\t)\n159\t\n160\tN_GENES = sum(n for _, n, _ in SPEC)\n161\tN_CATS = np.concatenate([np.full(n, k, dtype=np.int64) for _, n, k in SPEC])\n162\t\n163\t_OFFSETS = {}\n164\t_o = 0\n165\tfor _name, _n, _k in SPEC:\n166\t    _OFFSETS[_name] = (_o, _o + _n)\n167\t    _o += _n\n168\t\n169\t\n170\tdef gene_slice(name):\n171\t    \"\"\"(start, stop) index of a named gene block inside a genome vector.\"\"\"\n172\t    return _OFFSETS[name]\n173\t\n174\t\n175\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n176\tclass Plan:\n177\t    \"\"\"A decoded population of plans, every field already expanded to ``[N, ...]``.\n178\t\n179\t    Held as arrays rather than objects because the executor drives the whole population -- and\n180\t    every replicate of it -- in one pass over the batch dimension.\n181\t    \"\"\"\n182\t\n183\t    crop_of: np.ndarray        # [N, TILES] int8, -1 = fallow\n184\t    structure_of: np.ndarray   # [N, TILES] int8, NO_STRUCTURE / COOP / PASTURE\n185\t    phase: np.ndarray          # [N, TILES] int8\n186\t    stop_plant: np.ndarray     # [N] int16\n187\t    harvest_par: np.ndarray    # [N, N_CROPS] int8, index into HARVEST_DELTA/ONGOING_THRESHOLD\n188\t    hires: np.ndarray          # [N, SEASON_DAYS] int16, target headcount\n189\t    sell_qty: np.ndarray       # [N, N_BLOCKS, N_CROPS] int32\n190\t    sell_every: np.ndarray     # [N] int16\n191\t    floor_frac: np.ndarray     # [N, N_CROPS] float64\n192\t    seed_reserve: np.ndarray   # [N] float64\n193\t    land_day: np.ndarray       # [N, 3] int16\n194\t    dump_day: np.ndarray       # [N] int16\n195\t    herd: np.ndarray           # [N, N_ANIMALS] int16, target headcount per species\n196\t    stock_day: np.ndarray      # [N] int16, day to start buying animals (NEVER = none)\n197\t    feed_wheat: np.ndarray     # [N] int16, wheat units held per animal for FEED\n198\t    fert_policy: np.ndarray    # [N] int8, FERT_SELL / FERT_APPLY / FERT_APPLY_THEN_SELL\n199\t\n200\t    @property\n201\t    def size(self):\n202\t        return self.crop_of.shape[0]\n203\t\n204\t    def repeat(self, times):\n205\t        \"\"\"Tile the population ``times`` over, so each plan gets several replicate games.\n206\t\n207\t        Iterates the dataclass fields rather than naming each one, because the hand-written version\n208\t        listed eleven fields and adding a twelfth meant every replicate silently carried a default.\n209\t        \"\"\"\n210\t        return Plan(*(np.repeat(getattr(self, f), times, axis=0)\n211\t                      for f in self.__dataclass_fields__))\n212\t\n213\t    def take(self, index):\n214\t        \"\"\"Select plans by index array, in order.\"\"\"\n215\t        index = np.asarray(index)\n216\t        return Plan(*(getattr(self, f.name)[index] for f in self.__dataclass_fields__.values()))\n217\t\n218\t\n219\tdef decode(genomes):\n220\t    \"\"\"Turn ``[N, N_GENES]`` categorical genomes into a :class:`Plan`.\"\"\"\n221\t    g = np.asarray(genomes, dtype=np.int64)\n222\t    if g.ndim == 1:\n223\t        g = g[None, :]\n224\t    n = g.shape[0]\n225\t\n226\t    def block(name):\n227\t        lo, hi = _OFFSETS[name]\n228\t        return g[:, lo:hi]\n229\t\n230\t    raw_use = block(\"crop_of\")                       # 0 -> FALLOW, 1..5 -> crop, 6 coop, 7 pasture\n231\t    crop = raw_use - 1\n232\t    structure = np.where(raw_use == CROP_GENE_COOP, COOP,\n233\t                         np.where(raw_use == CROP_GENE_PASTURE, PASTURE, NO_STRUCTURE))\n234\t    # A structure tile grows nothing. Without this the executor would try to plant a pasture, which\n235\t    # is the class of bug that left `jax_frontier` with `max empty pens: 0` and $380 in the bank.\n236\t    crop = np.where(structure != NO_STRUCTURE, FALLOW, crop)\n237\t    sell = SELL_QTYS[block(\"sell_qty\")].reshape(n, N_BLOCKS, N_CROPS)\n238\t    return Plan(\n239\t        crop_of=crop.astype(np.int8),\n240\t        structure_of=structure.astype(np.int8),\n241\t        phase=block(\"phase\").astype(np.int8),\n242\t        stop_plant=STOP_PLANT_DAYS[block(\"stop_plant\")[:, 0]],\n243\t        harvest_par=block(\"harvest_par\").astype(np.int8),\n244\t        hires=HIRE_LEVELS[block(\"hires\")],\n245\t        sell_qty=sell,\n246\t        sell_every=SELL_EVERY[block(\"sell_every\")[:, 0]],\n247\t        floor_frac=FLOOR_FRACS[block(\"floor_frac\")],\n248\t        seed_reserve=SEED_RESERVES[block(\"seed_reserve\")[:, 0]],\n249\t        land_day=LAND_DAYS[block(\"land_day\")],\n250\t        dump_day=DUMP_DAYS[block(\"dump_day\")[:, 0]],\n251\t        herd=HERD_LEVELS[block(\"herd\")],\n252\t        stock_day=STOCK_DAYS[block(\"stock_day\")[:, 0]],\n253\t        feed_wheat=FEED_WHEAT[block(\"feed_wheat\")[:, 0]],\n254\t        fert_policy=block(\"fert_policy\")[:, 0].astype(np.int8),\n255\t    )\n256\t\n257\t\n258\tdef herd_gene(count):\n259\t    \"\"\"The gene value whose `HERD_LEVELS` entry is `count`, for writing a known plan down by hand.\"\"\"\n260\t    hits = np.flatnonzero(HERD_LEVELS == count)\n261\t    if not len(hits):\n262\t        raise ValueError(f\"herd size {count} is not on the ladder {list(HERD_LEVELS)}\")\n263\t    return int(hits[0])\n264\t\n265\t\n266\tdef pad_genome(genome):\n267\t    \"\"\"Migrate a genome saved before a block was appended, by filling the new genes with category 0.\n268\t\n269\t    `kagg/search/best_plan.json` holds a 277-gene genome from before the livestock blocks existed and\n270\t    `agent.py` loads it as the default plan. Category 0 is `HERD_LEVELS[0] == 0` and\n271\t    `FEED_WHEAT[0] == 0`, i.e. no animals -- so a padded old genome decodes to exactly the plan it\n272\t    always decoded to, which is what `tests/test_plan_animals.py` asserts.\n273\t\n274\t    A genome that is *longer* than `N_GENES` is an error rather than something to truncate: it means\n275\t    the file was written by a newer version and silently dropping its tail would run a different plan\n276\t    than the one that was saved.\n277\t    \"\"\"\n278\t    g = np.asarray(genome, dtype=np.int64)\n279\t    if g.shape[-1] > N_GENES:\n280\t        raise ValueError(f\"genome has {g.shape[-1]} genes, more than the current {N_GENES}\")\n281\t    if g.shape[-1] == N_GENES:\n282\t        return g\n283\t    pad = np.zeros(g.shape[:-1] + (N_GENES - g.shape[-1],), dtype=np.int64)\n284\t    return np.concatenate([g, pad], axis=-1)\n285\t\n286\t\n287\t# **Livestock is in the sampled space.** Set False only to reproduce a crop-only search.\n288\t#\n289\t# A hand-written plan with 14 animals and `feed_wheat = 2` on the $3,000 opening stake went bankrupt\n290\t# ($7 banked, 5 FEEDs a season against the leaders' 319). That is one point in the space, not a verdict\n291\t# on the space -- the herd size, the stocking day and the feed reserve are exactly what the search is\n292\t# for, and it will find that a big herd on a small stake starves. Gating the genes off because one\n293\t# hand-picked setting failed would have hidden the search's own answer.\n294\tLIVESTOCK_ENABLED = True\n295\t\n296\t\n297\tdef uniform_probs():\n298\t    \"\"\"The initial CEM distribution: uniform over every gene's categories.\n299\t\n300\t    Deliberately uninformed. Seeding it from the heuristic would reintroduce exactly the prior the\n301\t    search exists to escape.\n302\t    \"\"\"\n303\t    k = int(N_CATS.max())\n304\t    probs = np.zeros((N_GENES, k))\n305\t    for i, cats in enumerate(N_CATS):\n306\t        probs[i, :cats] = 1.0 / cats\n307\t    if not LIVESTOCK_ENABLED:\n308\t        # Zero the structure categories and every non-zero herd level, then renormalise. The genes\n309\t        # still exist and still decode -- they are simply never drawn, so tonight's run searches the\n310\t        # same crop-only space that produced $42,087 rather than exploring a half the executor cannot\n311\t        # yet keep alive. See the flag's comment for the measurement.\n312\t        lo, hi = _OFFSETS[\"crop_of\"]\n313\t        probs[lo:hi, CROP_GENE_COOP] = 0.0\n314\t        probs[lo:hi, CROP_GENE_PASTURE] = 0.0\n315\t        for name in (\"herd\", \"stock_day\", \"feed_wheat\", \"fert_policy\"):\n316\t            a, b = _OFFSETS[name]\n317\t            probs[a:b, :] = 0.0\n318\t            probs[a:b, 0] = 1.0\n319\t        probs[lo:hi] /= probs[lo:hi].sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n320\t    return probs\n321\t\n322\t\n323\tdef leaders_genome():\n324\t    \"\"\"The plan the top of the leaderboard actually runs, encoded as a genome.\n325\t\n326\t    Measured from replay JSON in `docs/episode_teardown_2026-08-03.md`, not guessed.\n327\t    `Dmitry Larko` (#1), `IY` (#4) and `Juste Me` (#5) submit byte-identical integers:\n328\t\n329\t        14 BUILD_PASTURE, 0 BUILD_COOP\n330\t        seeds  MELON 21   STRAWBERRY 44   WHEAT 66\n331\t        animals COW 8   SHEEP 6   GOOSE 0\n332\t        306 hires over 30 days (~12 hands rebuilt every dawn), 2 land, 967 wheat bought as feed\n333\t        cows from day 0; land on days 7 and 10\n334\t\n335\t    **This is a starting point for search, not an answer.** The whole reason to have it is that CEM\n336\t    around a known-good plan beats CEM from uniform: we already know these numbers work, so the\n337\t    question is only whether better ones exist nearby.\n338\t\n339\t    The *spatial* layout is ours, not theirs -- the counts are measured but which tile holds what is\n340\t    not extracted yet, so pastures go nearest the shed and the crops fill outward in serpentine order.\n341\t    CEM will move them.\n342\t    \"\"\"\n343\t    g = np.zeros(N_GENES, dtype=np.int64)\n344\t\n345\t    use = np.zeros(TILES, dtype=np.int64)\n346\t    use[:14] = CROP_GENE_PASTURE\n347\t    use[14:35] = 5                                    # gene 5 -> MELON  (21 tiles)\n348\t    use[35:79] = 4                                    # gene 4 -> STRAWBERRY (44 tiles)\n349\t    lo, hi = _OFFSETS[\"crop_of\"]\n350\t    g[lo:hi] = use\n351\t\n352\t    def put(name, value):\n353\t        a, b = _OFFSETS[name]\n354\t        g[a:b] = value\n355\t\n356\t    put(\"phase\", 0)\n357\t    put(\"stop_plant\", int(np.argmin(np.abs(STOP_PLANT_DAYS - 20))))\n358\t    put(\"harvest_par\", 2)                             # HARVEST_DELTA 0: cut at max_yield_day\n359\t    put(\"hires\", int(np.argmin(np.abs(HIRE_LEVELS - 12))))\n360\t    put(\"sell_every\", int(np.argmin(np.abs(SELL_EVERY - 2))))\n361\t    put(\"floor_frac\", 1)                              # 0.3 of base: sell unless the price collapses\n362\t    put(\"seed_reserve\", 1)                            # keep $200 back\n363\t    put(\"dump_day\", int(np.argmin(np.abs(DUMP_DAYS - 26))))\n364\t    put(\"herd\", 0)\n365\t    put(\"stock_day\", 0)                               # cows on day 0, as measured\n366\t    put(\"feed_wheat\", int(np.argmin(np.abs(FEED_WHEAT - 2))))\n367\t    put(\"fert_policy\", FERT_APPLY_THEN_SELL)\n368\t\n369\t    a, b = _OFFSETS[\"herd\"]\n370\t    g[a] = 0                                          # GOOSE 0\n371\t    g[a + 1] = herd_gene(8)                           # COW 8\n372\t    g[a + 2] = herd_gene(6)                           # SHEEP 6\n373\t\n374\t    a, b = _OFFSETS[\"land_day\"]\n375\t    g[a] = int(np.argmin(np.abs(LAND_DAYS - 6)))      # first extra quadrant ~day 7\n376\t    g[a + 1] = int(np.argmin(np.abs(LAND_DAYS - 9)))  # second ~day 10\n377\t    g[a + 2] = len(LAND_DAYS) - 1                     # NEVER\n378\t\n379\t    a, b = _OFFSETS[\"sell_qty\"]\n380\t    g[a:b] = 3                                        # SELL_QTYS 10 per tick per product per block\n381\t    return g\n382\t\n383\t\n384\tdef probs_around(genome, concentration=0.55):\n385\t    \"\"\"A CEM start distribution centred on `genome`, with the rest spread over the alternatives.\n386\t\n387\t    `concentration` is the probability mass on the seeded category; the remainder is uniform over the\n388\t    others so every value stays reachable. This is the difference between \"search from scratch\" and\n389\t    \"search around what we know works\" -- `uniform_probs` throws away the only measurement we have of\n390\t    a plan that banks $100k+.\n391\t\n392\t    Deliberately not 1.0. A point start collapses CEM's first generation to one plan and it can only\n393\t    walk downhill from there; `CLAUDE.md` records the winner's curse biting at +40% reported against\n394\t    +12.8% held out, and keeping mass on the alternatives is what leaves room to escape it.\n395\t    \"\"\"\n396\t    g = np.asarray(pad_genome(genome), dtype=np.int64)\n397\t    k = int(N_CATS.max())\n398\t    probs = np.zeros((N_GENES, k))\n399\t    for i, cats in enumerate(N_CATS):\n400\t        cats = int(cats)\n401\t        if cats == 1:\n402\t            probs[i, 0] = 1.0\n403\t            continue\n404\t        rest = (1.0 - concentration) / (cats - 1)\n405\t        probs[i, :cats] = rest\n406\t        probs[i, int(g[i])] = concentration\n407\t    return probs\n408\t\n409\t\n410\tdef sample(probs, n, rng):\n411\t    \"\"\"Draw ``n`` genomes from a factored categorical distribution ``[N_GENES, K]``.\"\"\"\n412\t    cum = np.cumsum(probs, axis=1)\n413\t    cum /= cum[:, -1:]\n414\t    draws = rng.random((n, probs.shape[0]))\n415\t    out = (draws[:, :, None] > cum[None, :, :]).sum(axis=2)\n416\t    return np.minimum(out, (N_CATS - 1)[None, :]).astype(np.int64)\n417\t\n418\t\n419\tdef random_genomes(n, rng):\n420\t    return sample(uniform_probs(), n, rng)\n421\t\n422\t\n423\tdef describe(genome):\n424\t    \"\"\"A human-readable summary of one genome, for the experiment log.\"\"\"\n425\t    plan = decode(genome[None, :])\n426\t    lines = []\n427\t    # **Structures are counted, not folded into FALLOW.** A pasture tile decodes to `crop_of == FALLOW`\n428\t    # by design, so the first version of this reported 14 pastures as \"FALLOW=35\" and never mentioned\n429\t    # the herd at all -- a livestock plan and a crop plan printed identically. Third instance today of\n430\t    # a census dropping a column and the zero being read as a fact.\n431\t    counts = {}\n432\t    for c, st in zip(plan.crop_of[0], plan.structure_of[0]):\n433\t        if st == COOP:\n434\t            name = \"COOP\"\n435\t        elif st == PASTURE:\n436\t            name = \"PASTURE\"\n437\t        else:\n438\t            name = \"FALLOW\" if c < 0 else CROP_NAMES[c]\n439\t        counts[name] = counts.get(name, 0) + 1\n440\t    lines.append(\"tiles: \" + \", \".join(f\"{k}={v}\" for k, v in sorted(counts.items())))\n441\t    herd = \", \".join(f\"{n} {int(v)}\" for n, v in zip(ANIMAL_NAMES, plan.herd[0]) if v)\n442\t    fert = [\"sell\", \"apply\", \"apply then sell\"][int(plan.fert_policy[0])]\n443\t    lines.append(f\"herd: {herd or 'none'} | stock from day {int(plan.stock_day[0])}\"\n444\t                 f\" | feed {int(plan.feed_wheat[0])} wheat/animal | fertiliser: {fert}\")\n445\t    lines.append(f\"stop_plant day {int(plan.stop_plant[0])}, dump day {int(plan.dump_day[0])}\")\n446\t    lines.append(\"hires by day: \" + \" \".join(str(int(h)) for h in plan.hires[0]))\n447\t    lines.append(f\"sell every {int(plan.sell_every[0])}h, reserve ${plan.seed_reserve[0]:.0f}\")\n448\t    for c, name in enumerate(CROP_NAMES):\n449\t        qty = \" \".join(str(int(q)) for q in plan.sell_qty[0, :, c])\n450\t        lines.append(\n451\t            f\"  {name:<11} sell/tick by block: {qty}\"\n452\t            f\" | floor {plan.floor_frac[0, c]:.1f}x base\"\n453\t            f\" | harvest par {int(plan.harvest_par[0, c])}\"\n454\t        )\n455\t    lines.append(\"land days: \" + \" \".join(str(int(d)) for d in plan.land_day[0]))\n456\t    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n457\t"}]},"uuid":"b477ac05-1a83-4016-93d0-976c7d725319","timestamp":"2026-08-04T09:47:00.922Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2bde52c7-3402-498e-a400-7c39f40e10a0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"6e91a562-2c58-4e1d-8c6f-dd43634ac84a","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"humming-hopping-marshmallow"}
