{"parentUuid":"e2c110d8-0e2a-4f08-82ed-c1d365f17fcb","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"af0fe4f4b572e87a8","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5","id":"msg_011CdhWFdkCAUdTCmsGFgLbZ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read `plan.py`, `executor.py`, `cem.py`, `rollout.py`, `replay_plan.py`, `test_replay_plan.py`, `mine_build_orders.py`, `episode_teardown_2026-08-03.md`, and `best_plan.json` in full. Ran the actual test suite and re-derived the layout from the raw episode JSON myself rather than trusting either your transcription or mine. Findings below, then the three answers.\n\n**Independent verification of your layout read.** `PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m pytest tests/test_replay_plan.py -v` — 5/5 green, genuinely, not just claimed. I then called `replay_plan.layout_from_replay(\"data/89113699.json\", seat=1)` directly and printed the raw `pasture`/`crops` coordinates myself: 28 pasture, 0 coop, `{'MELON': 12, 'CARROT': 9}`, `land_days=(10,)`, `dump_day=20`, `animal_counts={'SHEEP': 16, 'COW': 12}`, `wheat_total=3820`. Rendered into a grid from that data, it matches your ASCII cell-for-cell (crop block top-left, pasture block filling outward, one idle tile at (9,0)). My first instinct was that your \".\" at (9,0) contradicted \"only y<5 locked\" since (9,0) is NE and NE was bought — but that's not a locked tile, it's an unlocked-but-idle one; your legend text (\"`.` = never unlocked\") was imprecise for that one cell but the underlying data is right. Your reading holds up against the raw JSON, not just against your own script.\n\n## 1. No, I don't still hold §5(d) — and I now know why I was wrong\n\nI ran `scripts/mine_build_orders.py --top 20` myself rather than trusting the aggregate. Every single one of the top 20 seats runs a substantial animal portfolio (smallest is 4 SHEEP + 2 COW; several run 16-31 per species) with FEED counts in the hundreds. There is no crop-heavy seat in the top 20 — I looked, and it isn't there.\n\nMore important: my §5(d) argument rested on a mechanical claim I got wrong. I had said ranch pens cap at 4 because \"PICKUP/DROP/shed-PLACE\" require shed-adjacency (`reference/kaggriculture.py:117-124`), and I extended that to mean pens themselves must sit on a shed-access tile. Re-reading the `PLACE` op just now (ref 447-478): the **animal-placement branch has no shed-adjacency check at all** — it only requires standing on a matching empty COOP/PASTURE tile, anywhere on unlocked land. `FEED`/`CARE`/`HARVEST` (ref 480-505) likewise only check the unit's own tile. Shed-adjacency gates *only* PICKUP, DROP, and the shed-drop branch of PLACE — i.e. fetching wheat and depositing product, not building or working a pen. `kagg/search/executor.py` is built around exactly this: it treats the shed tiles as a once-a-day \"dawn PICKUP window\" (`executor.py:379-416`, `SHED_ORDS`) and then routes units out to whatever tile their job is on, animal or crop, identically. The 28-pasture layout is the proof — it's scattered across the whole unlocked NE block, not clustered at `(4,4)`.\n\nSo the premise under §5(d) (\"animals cap at 4, crops don't\") is false. Once corrected, animals scale by tile count exactly like crops, and the measured evidence says winners commit *more* of that scaled tile budget to pasture than to crop (28 vs 21 on the one fully-read seat; PLANT ops lower and FEED/CARE/COLLECT_FERTILIZER far higher for top-12 vs bottom-12 in aggregate). I was directionally backwards. Retracted.\n\n## 2. Seed with `probs_around(replay_genome)` — and separately widen `HERD_LEVELS`. Both, for different reasons\n\nThese are two different problems and I'd fix both, not pick one.\n\n**Why CEM found the crop basin at all**, mechanically: `cem.py`'s own docstring is explicit that this is a factored categorical / product-of-marginals model (`cem.py:14-19`), chosen deliberately over a GA because \"the genome is a set of independent categorical choices with no meaningful locality between genes.\" That's true for `crop_of` (one tile committing to MELON pays off on its own, needing only the shared `phase`/`stop_plant`/`harvest_par` genes to be roughly sane). It is **not** true for the ranch: a `crop_of` tile set to PASTURE only pays off if it *also* co-occurs, in the same sampled genome, with a matching `herd` target, an early `stock_day`, and enough `feed_wheat`/`hires` to keep the loop fed — a four-block conjunction. Under i.i.d. sampling from independent marginals, the probability of drawing that whole conjunction together is the product of four small probabilities, so most samples that touch PASTURE land with mismatched herd/stock genes, score *worse* than a same-generation crop plan (empty pastures are pure opportunity cost against a crop that would have used the same tile), and the elite-update pushes PASTURE's marginal probability *down* rather than up. This is not \"not enough generations\" — it's the standard EDA failure mode for conjunctive fitness, the same \"deceptive valley\" shape as the sub-action-search wall your `routines.py` was built to route around, just recurring one level up in a different search algorithm. So: yes, seed with `probs_around(replay_genome)`. That's the correct fix for this specific failure — it plants the conjunction directly instead of asking a product-of-marginals sampler to discover it by luck, and `test_the_seed_is_a_distribution_not_an_answer` already guards that this stays a soft prior (every gene keeps positive mass on every alternative), so it doesn't relitigate the shape-vs-policy line.\n\n**But seeding alone won't reach the actual leaders**, because of a second, independent problem: `HERD_LEVELS = [0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16]` (`plan.py:119`) tops out at 16 per species. My own top-20 pull shows winning seats running 24, 25, 28, 30, 31 *per species* (episode 89084587: 30 SHEEP + 31 COW; 89072564: 25 + 25). The genome cannot express the actual best-known points — not \"hasn't found them yet,\" *cannot represent them*, full stop. That's a hard ladder cap, not a search-dynamics issue, and seeding won't fix it: CEM will converge nicely around the replay genome and then sit at herd=16, silently capped below what's measured to work. Widen the ladder (e.g. append 20/24/28/32) before or alongside seeding.\n\nI'd push back on the other half of your hypothesis, though: **`HIRE_LEVELS` topping at 14 is not the bottleneck.** The measured seat runs crew of 12 for most of the season, rising to 13 near the end (`crew_by_day` I printed above), and `HIRE_LEVELS` max is 14 — that already covers it, with only a minor quantization gap (13 isn't itself a rung, it rounds to 12 or 14). Top-20 aggregate hires/day average 8.0-11.1, well inside range. I don't think hires needs touching; herd does.\n\n## 3. My §3 proposal was a cruder, and in one respect wrong, version of what's already built\n\n`executor.decide` already does \"keep retrying every turn after the gate day, not once\": `buy_land = (view.n_land < plan.land_day.shape[1]) & (day >= plan.land_day[games, slot])` (`executor.py:459-460`) is recomputed every turn inside `play`'s per-step loop, so once a parcel's `land_day` arrives, the order is re-emitted every single turn until `n_land` increments — free, exactly as I argued, for exactly the reason I gave (`_do_buy_land` no-ops for free). So that part of my proposal is confirmed correct and is already implemented, just parameterized by a searchable per-parcel gate day rather than hardcoded to turn 0.\n\nWhere I was wrong: my original framing was \"an evergreen standing order for land, full stop\" — I hadn't separated \"guarantee the attempt is never silently dropped\" from \"attempt all three parcels unconditionally.\" Those are different claims, and the second one is false. I pulled the top-20 land days myself: `10` / `11` / `10` / `11` / `10` / `11` / `10` / `7,13` / `7,10` / `10` / `7,10` / `9,12` / `10` / `11` / `11` / `11,15` / `10` / `7,11` / `6,10` / `11` — roughly half the top-20 buy exactly one parcel, the other half buy two, and none of the top 20 buy all three. So \"how many parcels\" is a genuine, seat-varying strategic choice among winners, not a mistake losers make — and a standing order that unconditionally chased all three would have spent $7,000 (or even the second parcel's $2,000) that these seats deliberately declined. `land_day`'s design — three independent genes, each with its own earliest-day ladder including `NEVER` — is exactly right for this: it lets search decide *whether* and *when* per parcel, while the decoder guarantees that once \"yes\" is decided, the attempt is never forgotten. That's the correct division (search owns the decision, decoder owns not-losing-it), and it's already there. I'd change nothing about it."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4907,"cache_read_input_tokens":167490,"output_tokens":12947,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4907},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":12947,"cache_read_input_tokens":167490,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4907,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4907,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdhWFcEfGgXAPWJHJuTpJ","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"34501405-6a3b-4040-b7a5-df21ba6fe380","timestamp":"2026-08-04T09:56:40.952Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"6e91a562-2c58-4e1d-8c6f-dd43634ac84a","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"humming-hopping-marshmallow"}
